ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
企業コメント概要
0 10 20 30

金利 × 設備投資
価格転嫁×企業収益
AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に

―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？
Q2. 先行きの金利上昇の「質」を左右するポイントは何か？
Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？
問われるのは「良い金利上昇」か
「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

Taiwan Semis: GUC (3443.TW): Mgmt. Call takeaways: Server CPU ramp 
to sustain momentum into 2027; opportunities across applications
31 October 2026 | 12:35AM HKT
We talked to GUC (3443.TW, not covered) management recently. The company’s Sep 
revenues were up 22% MoM (vs. +1% MoM in Aug), reflecting its revenues from CPU 
of a leading US CSP in strong ramp up with more foundry capacity rolling out, and 
would continue to support the growth in 4Q26 and 2027 per the company. The 
positive tone echoes our positive view on Global Servers TAM, which we expect 
leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will grow their capex budgets 
by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026-28E, with our estimates being raised by 4% / 17% / 
22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E. Revenues in 3Q26 are driven by both 
NRE and turnkey solutions, with turnkey solutions revenues up 44% QoQ on the CPU 
ramp up, and despite turnkey solutions up to ~90% of 3Q26 revenues, the GM 
sustained at 20%+ in 3Q26, thanks to higher-margin NRE milestones. GUC adjusts 
pricing to reflect rising wafer and memory costs, and management sees memory 
impact is manageable on its customers’ projects. On competition, management 
highlighted its position as a core partner of TSMC, with in-house IP for 3D stacking as 
a key differentiator. GUC’s strong turnkey pull-in from its CSP customer’s server CPU 
and its focus on IP for TSMC 3D stacking point to continued demand for TSMC’s 
(2330.TW, Buy) advanced nodes and packaging. The positive tone on ASIC demand 
also echoes our positive view on rising ASIC penetration rate (~60% by 2028E, report 
link), supported by leading CSPs diversifying thier AI chips platforms and reducing 
token per second per costs, driving inferencing / training efficiency.

Company profile: GUC (3443.TW, not covered) is a ASIC design service company 
and a TSMC affiliate, providing NRE design services and turnkey production for

### Page 2

custom chips manufactured at TSMC. Its customers span cloud (CPUs, AI accelerators,
BMCs), automotive and consumer (image sensors, SSD controllers), with 68% of its
products under 7nm.
Key takeaways
1. Mass production as the key profit driver: Management noted that the key to
profitability lies in converting NRE projects into mass production. With the largest
customer’s CPU now in volume production, turnkey has become the main growth driver.
NRE carries higher GM than turnkey, but as it is recognized by milestone, quarterly NRE

margin varies with the mix of projects at different stages.
2. Expanding opportunities across cloud and automotive: GUC continues to open up
new opportunities across applications. In cloud, it works with all major US CSPs on
projects spanning CPUs and GPUs and also supplies other server projects such as BMCs,
while actively pursuing further AI accelerator opportunities. In automotive, it is engaged
with both US and Chinese customers at the NRE stage, two of which already plan for

mass production.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Idea
Axion CPU demand remains strong and on track: We noted  in a prior report  that
demand for Google's Axion CPUs, which GUC helps design, remains robust.
However, we now see further upside in both 2027 and 2028 from our previous
forecast. Also, some investors have questioned whether the Axion3 schedule has
been pushed out. Our  recent supply chain checks suggest that Axion3 has already
reached wafer-in and is on track for wafer-out in mid-2027. Axion CPU could reach 4-

5mn units of  Axion2 equivalent CPU in 2027, given that Axion3 includes two
computing dies but also adopts TSMC 3nm process. We now forecast volume at
4mn units of Axion2 and 500k units of Axion3 in 2027, then 3mn units of Axion3 in
2028. We project ASP at US$700 and US$1400, respectively.
We also see upside surprise from Cobalt CPU: We believe the CPU demand upside
is coming not just from Axion but also Microsoft's Cobalt CPU. GUC continues to
get more front-end wafer support from TSMC; given strong CPU demand, we now

forecast >1mn units of Cobalt 200 in 2027 with ASP of ~US$700. Our industry
checks also suggest that Cobalt 300 is on track for mass production in 2028, which
might adopt TSMC's 2nm process. By assuming it could also be a dual-computing-die
CPU, we currently forecast the ASP to be ~US$1800 with 500k units in 2028.
3Q26 revenue beat from CPU customers' demand pull-forward and new
customer: The company posted its  3Q26 results: revenue beat our estimate

strongly (by 23%);  the upside comes mostly from the largest CPU customer's pulling
demand forward  (50-55% of 3Q26 total revenue) and also the new US AI customer,
which has adopted the 5nm process and SoW (system-on-wafer) at TSMC. In 4Q26,
we expect a major Chinese auto company to start to contribute revenue. With the
ongoing robust CPU demand, we now expect 4Q26 revenue to be up 20% QoQ. See
Exhibit 3  for our 3Q26 earnings review.
Reiterate OW and raise price target to NT$8600: This 37% increase mostly

reflects the upside we have seen from CPU demand. However, aside from CPU
demand, we also see strong revenue from Tesla, Meta, Horizon, etc.   in 2028. Thus,
we expect 2025-28 revenue CAGR to reach 120%. Our new price target implies P/E
of 52x for 2027e and 27x for 2028e, which we think is justified by its strong revenue
growth.

Idea
2
Strong chip demand from CPU  and AI business
Axion CPU should be the largest customer for GUC in 2026-2028: We therefore now
forecast only 1.5mn units of Google Axion2 CPU in 2026, given the demand pull-in. By
assuming chip ASP at ~US$700, we now see 2026 Google CPU contributing slightly more
than US$1000mn.
For 2027, we have been seeing strong rises in demand expectations for Axion CPU,
including  the ramp of the next-generation Axion3. We now expect Axion CPU revenue of

close to US$3.5bn, including 4mn units of Axion2 and 500k units of Axion3. In addition,
we also expect the chip ASP of Axion3 to double from Axion2 given the increase of 3nm
computing dies from one to two.
For 2028, we now forecast  3mn units of Axion3, slightly lower than Axion2's annual run
rate of 4mn units, mostly also driven by the doubling of computing dies.
CPU turnkey revenue carries a low gross margin for Axion CPU – likely around 10% –
because this is a “Turnkey 2 or 3” project, where GUC provides advanced packaging design

support (e.g., silicon interposer and ABF substrates) but does not own the photomasks for
the ASIC designs. We also expect margin for Axion3 to be slightly lower than for Axion2,
given higher chip ASP or larger project size.
Cobalt demand could  also grow 3-4x in 2027: GUC also helps with Microsoft's Cobalt
200/300 CPU. We now forecast at least US$1bn of GUC revenue contribution from
Cobalt 200 and Maia 200 in 2027. Also, our industry checks also suggest that Cobalt 300

is on track for mass production in 2028, which might adopt TSMC's 2nm process. By
assuming it could also be a dual-computing-die CPU, we currently forecast the ASP to be
~US$1800 with 500k units in 2028.
Meta ASIC (MTIA 600): In our AI supply chain tracker, we noted our belief that GUC
could win one of the Meta ASIC projects, led by the Rivos team, and we now think it might
be MTIA 600, based on supply chain checks. The AI ASIC targets tape-out in 1H27, with

potential CoWoS out in 2028.
Note: these forecasts are from Asia Pacific research and incorporate supply chain and
industry checks.

4
Estimate revision summary
We raise   our EPS estimates 17% for 2026, 18% for 2027, and 50% for 2028: Our
increases come mostly from the strength in CPU as well as other AI projects' contribution,
including Meta ASIC, US design house adopting SoW, etc. With turnkey revenue share
seen increasing in both 2027 and 2028, we now assume that gross margin will fall from
21.4% in 2026 to 15.4% in 2028. However, we expect firmer operating margin this year,

thanks to a policy change regarding employee bonus; although this assumption dips in
2027, it rebounds in 2028 because of larger revenue scale with better operating leverage.
The same trend is visible in  net margin.

Morgan Stanley Research 5
Price target rises 37%, to NT$8,600
This reflects our 2027-28 estimate revisions. Although we are cutting our terminal growth
rate assumption from 5% to 4.5% because of margin dilution, our other  key valuation
assumptions are unchanged, including  cost of equity of 9.2% (a beta of 1.2, a risk premium
of 6%, and a risk-free rate of 2%), and intermediate growth rate  of 14.5%.
Our bull and bear case values rise by similar magnitudes, to   NT$11,680  and NT$5,300,

respectively.

Relative valuation
Our price target of NT$8,600 implies 52x our 2027 EPS estimate, around +1 SD from
GUC’s historical NTM P/E since 2013. We maintain our view that the design service market

will proliferate with AI ASIC outgrowing AI GPU in the long term, and therefore believe
that further re-rating would be justified with project wins likely to come. Despite slower
recovery of non-AI business, we continue to appreciate GUC’s growing exposure to cloud
AI semis (including AI accelerators, AI server CPU, and BMC chips).

remains stable. A few new leading-edge

shipments. TSMC's leading-edge tape-out

well as the auto chip and SSD controller
projects.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 172: 261105_daiwa_nypcb

Source PDF: all report pdf/261105_daiwa_nypcb.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: NYPCB released its 3Q26 results after market hours on 5 November. Net profit 
was TWD3.30bn (EPS: TWD5.10), 9% below our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus. 
Revenue rose 21% QoQ and 49% YoY to TWD16.38bn, in line with expectations. GM was 
29.3%, 1.0ppt below our estimate and 0.7ppt below consensus, reflecting yield losses 
during the Kunshan ramp-up and a more modest BT substrate margin uplift than we had 
assumed. OPM was 26.4%, versus our 27.5% estimate and 26.5% consensus. Despite 
operating profit being broadly in line, net profit was weighed down by a higher tax rate 
(26% vs. our 22% estimate), reflecting increased profitability at NYPCB’s China factories.

Looking ahead, we expect modest BT and c.10% ABF price hikes in 4Q26, which would 
result in mid-teens % QoQ growth in 4Q26E. This is consistent with its October revenue of 
TWD6.29bn (released 2 November, up 13% MoM and 70% YoY), equivalent to 34% of our 
4Q26 forecast (historic range being 33-39%). Per management, the Kunshan plant’s UTR 
has increased to 90-95% in October (vs. 80-85% in 3Q26 and 65-70% in 2Q26). As 
NYPCB is unwilling to accept LTAs with end clients (compared with its competitors), we 
believe its bargaining power should be the highest among TW substrate vendors. We have 
a Buy (1) rating on NYPCB, with a 12-month TP of TWD2,460, based on a target PER of 
49x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected 
demand for switches.

What’s the impact: 
Below is our first take on NYPCB’s 3Q26 results, released on 5 November.

• 3Q26 net profit missed expectations. Net profit came in at TWD3.30bn (EPS: 
TWD5.10), 9% below both our estimate (TWD3.63bn) and the Bloomberg consensus 
(TWD3.62bn). 3Q26 revenue of TWD16.38bn (up 21% QoQ and 49% YoY) was in-line 
with expectations, while the gross margin reached 29.3%, 1ppt/0.7ppt below 
our/Bloomberg consensus estimates due to loss on the yield rate as the Kunshan plant 
ramped up; our gross margin expansion assumption for BT substrate was also too 
bullish. Operating margin reached 26.4%, versus our 27.5% estimate and the street’s 
26.5%, mainly on the lower-than-expected gross margin. However, while the operating 
profit was largely on track with expectations, net profit was dragged down by a higher-
than-expected tax rate (26% vs. our 22% estimate) due to rising profitability from 
factories in China.

## Document 177: 261105_ml_kyec

Source PDF: all report pdf/261105_ml_kyec.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

King Yuan Electronics Corp.

Testing business diversification strengthen 
’27 outlook at low-end valuation

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 330.00 TWD | Price: 228.50 TWD

3Q26 results in line; 4Q TPU ramp offsets GPU headwind 
3Q26 sales were +9% QoQ backed by healthy demand from its GPU/CPU customers and 
early mobile inventory builds. With higher depreciation and labor cost moving to COGS 
as new facility production kick off, GM dipped 190bps QoQ to 37.6% in 3Q26 but 
operating leverage lifted OpM 40bps QoQ to 25.8%, taking EPS to NT$2.06. 4Q26 sales 
is guided +7-9% QoQ, in line with our preview, due to slower GPU ramp from continued 
HBM4 yield issue at Hynix partially offsetting strength in AI ASICs (Google’s v8t/i TPUs) 
and CPUs (Nvidia Vera, Google Axion and Microsoft Cobalt). Despite better business mix, 
the rising cost and slower UTR improvement should keep GMs stable at 36-37% in 4Q. 
Solid position into 2027 across chipset and wafer testing 
KYEC’s ’26 sales is now on track to grow 34% YoY and could further accelerate in ’27 at 
+53% YoY from a diversifying application base across GPU, CPU, networking switches and 
ASICs. It is also in a strong position to capture its foundry partner’s chip probing 
outsourcing demand in addition to higher testing unit/dollar content from its existing 
direct customer business. To support expansion across wafer sorting, chip testing and 
system level testing, the company is accelerating its clean room construction in Taiwan 
and has announced it will grow its testing footprint in the US. While its ’27 capex could 
stay at elevated levels, we think the investment mix shifting toward facility should keep its 
depreciation under control. This along with capex-light tool consignment business model 
could drive its GMs to 40%+ in 1H27 and keep profitability resilient through the cycles. 
Recent pullback to low-end valuation: particularly attractive 
We keep EPS for ‘26/27/28E largely unchanged at NT$7.87/NT$15.36/NT$19.18, and 
reiterate our Buy rating with PO of NT$330 still based on 18x 2H27-1H28E P/E (vs. its 
10-25x P/E range). Following the AI sentiment drag, the stock is trading at 15x/11x 
‘27/28E P/E, attractive as the business downside is protected by its leadership in scale 
and execution for AI chipset testing even if AI investment moderates in ‘28.

* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 7.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Founded in 1987 and headquartered in Hsinchu, Taiwan, 
King Yuan Electronics Corp. (KYEC) is a niche Outsourced 
Semiconductor Assembly and Test (OSAT) company 
focused on IC testing services, with market shares of 3% in 
OSAT (IC packaging+testing, number 7 vendor) and 14% in 
IC testing (number 2 vendor) globally. Its capacity is mainly 
located in Greater China. In addition to IC testing, it has 
c.20% revenue exposure to IC packaging, mainly at its 
Chinese subsidiary and mainly for memory products.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on KYEC, in view of undervalued 
upside from smartphone business for both near-term and 
longer-term, structural opportunities in AI areas led by GPU, 
as well as the solid GPM/ROE expansion toward into 2025.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
King Yuan Electronics Corp. (KYUFF) 
We value KYEC at NT$330 per share, based on 18x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is close to 
its historical average since 2010. In our view, P/E valuation is appropriate for KYEC given 
its strong growth in earnings over the past few years, future growth potentials with 
upside in total addressable market (TAM) and market share, and enhanced cash position. 
We think 18x P/E, which is above the average P/E level of global OSAT peers, is 
undemanding, in view of its better ROE profile and 2-3% dividend yield.

Downside risks are 
1) faster-than-expected pick-up by testing peers in high-end AI area, 
2) greater-than-expected slowdown in global smartphone/consumer electronics demand, 
3) potential asset disposal caused by strategy shift amid the ongoing decoupling in the 
semis supply chain.

### Page 1

WT Microelectronics

Datacenter continues to fuel growth amid 
a less favorable funding surrounding

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 270.00 TWD | Price: 195.00 TWD

3Q26 ahead on operating leverage  
3Q26 net income NT$10.3bn was 4% ahead of BofAe/consensus, thanks mainly to a 
slightly stronger-than-expected operating leverage. By end markets, the sequential 
revenue momentum turned out to be stronger-than-expected across auto, industrial, CE 
(consumer electronics), PC (personal computer) and smartphone verticals in 3Q26, due 
partly to the earlier pull-in which also will likely lead to a QoQ revenue drop in 4Q26 for 
these segments.  
Reaccelerating datacenter business into 4Q26 and 2027 
4Q26 revenue is guided to grow by 25+% QoQ based on the mid-point of guidance 
range, thanks mainly to the reacceleration in datacenter-related business, which more 
than offset the QoQ revenue decline in the segments mentioned above. Yet we expect 
the firm’s interest expense to notably increase in 4Q26 (vs 3Q26) to NT$3bn+, while 
management also attributes such increase to a higher funding cost (interest rate) 
environment and a bigger amount of working capital required to fuel future business 
growth. Looking into 2027, WT Micro expects continuous growth across datacenter, auto 
and industrial areas.   
Lower PO on a lowered multiple given higher interest rate 
We raise 2026-28E EPS by 7-11% after baking in the assumptions of stronger operating 
leverage post the release of 3Q26/4Q26 results/guidance, while our PO goes to NT$270 
(from NT$330) now based on 7x (was 10x) 2H27-1H28E P/E, at the low-to-mid-end of 
its historical 2011-25 trading range of 4-15x, we believe this level of PE is warranted 
because of a higher funding cost (interest rate at 4+%) environment into 2027-28 
balanced by a growing op margin, ROE and EPS CAGR over the forecast period. Given 
the still undemanding valuation at 6x/5x P/E and 11%/14% dividend yield in 2027/28E, 
we reiterate Buy rating on the stock, while staying confident for the company to record 
20+% top-line/bottom-line CAGR during 2026-28E.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
WT Microelectronics was founded in 1993 and went listed 
in 2002. The firm is the biggest distributor for 
semiconductor and technology hardware components 
globally with 10+% market share, and with more than 25k 
customers and over 400 suppliers. WT Micro operates in 
dual-headquarter business model, after acquiring Future 
Electronics in 2024, and now it has over eight thousand 
employees with over 160 sales branches.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on WT Micro, as we like the structural 
growth in datacenter business mainly driven by TPU, as well 
as the emerging benefits/synergies from the cross-selling 
with Future Electronics.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
WT Microelectronics (XZOPF) 
We base our PO NT$270 on 7x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at the low-to-mid-end of its 
historical 2011-25 trading range of 4-15x. This is justified in our view, given the higher 
interest rate balanced by the support from: 1) an operating margin of 2.2-2.3% in 2027-
28E, 2) improving ROE toward 33% in 2027-28E (vs 12% in 2024-25), and 3) 22% EPS 
CAGR during 2026-28E.

Upside risks to our PO are: 
1) faster-than-expected demand recovery in auto/industrial verticals, especially from 
US/Europe market 
2) a lower interest rate that could alleviate the company's financial burden at non-
operating level.

Downside risks to our PO are: 
1) a revenue decline in datacenter/server related business, which has been the key 
growth driver during 2021-24 
2) market share loss as suppliers or customers seek other distributors' help after WT 
Micro consolidates Future Electronics. 
3) Potential overhang from share dilution.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be

기아 (000270)

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY) 으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY) 으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY) 으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY) 을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000 원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 150,000원

## Document 223: [005380] 현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기, 물량 감소에도 확인된 이익 방어력 
현대차의 3Q26 매출액은 45조 1,100억원(Y oY -3.4%), 영업이익은 2조 6,770억원
(Y oY +5.5%), OPM 5.9%(Y oY +0.5%p)를 기록했다. 국내 파업 및 영업일수 감소 영
향으로 글로벌 도매 판매량은 92.1만대(Y oY -11.3%)까지 감소했으나, HEV 중심의 
고부가 차종 Mix 개선, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비(Y oY -1.2조원) 감소 효과
로 수익성은 오히려 개선됐다. 특히 HEV 판매 비중은 18.2%(Y oY +2.7%p)까지 상
승했다. 반면 EV 판매는 전년 대비 감소세가 이어지며 지역별 수요 둔화와 경쟁 심
화 영향이 지속됐다. 
연간 눈높이는 낮아졌지만, 믹스 개선은 지속 
현대차는 글로벌 수요 둔화와 생산 차질을 반영해 2026년 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 
기존 415.8만대에서 400만대로, 영업이익률 가이던스를 기존 6.3~7.3%에서 
5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정했다. 다만 북미를 중심으로 HEV 판매가 견조하게 유지되고 
있으며, 하반기 주요 신차 투입과 전동화 라인업 확대를 통해 믹스 개선 기조는 이
어질 전망이다. 중장기적으로는 로보택시 공급 확대와 Atlas 상용화 등 Physical AI 
사업도 계획대로 진행 중으로, 단기 판매 성장 둔화에도 자동차 본업의 수익성 방어
와 신사업 모멘텀은 유효한 것으로 판단한다. 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 460,000원 하향 
글로벌 완성차 업체들의 수익성 부담은 확대되고 있다. 3분기 자동차 부문 영업이익
률은 Mercedes-Benz 2.5%, Stellantis 0.7%, Volvo -5.6%, VW -7.8%를 기록했다. 
유럽 내 BEV 판매 확대에도 중국 전기차 업체들의 공격적인 해외 진출과 가격 경
쟁, 중국 시장 부진이 동시에 나타나면서 전통 OEM의 수익성 압박이 심화되고 있
다. 이러한 환경에서 현대차는 HEV 중심의 유연한 파워트레인 전략과 북미 중심의 
Mix 개선을 통해 5% 후반의 수익성을 유지하고 있다는 점이 상대적으로 부각된다.

## Document 232: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

## Document 244: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부_c581d324

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부_c581d324.pdf

### Page 1

삼성바이오로직스 (207940/KS) 
3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 
2026-10-06

제약/바이오. 이선경 / seonkyoung.lee@sks.co.kr / 3773-9089

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 2,000,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 1,354,000 원 
상승여력: 47.7%

Signal: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 전망  
Key: 5 공장 매출 본격화, 미국 공장 매출 기여에 따른 성장 기대   
Step: 단기 불확실성보다는 중장기적 성장 방향성에 주목

3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 성장지속, 컨센 부합 전망   
26년 3분기 연결기준 매출액은 1조 3,741억원(+9.3%YoY, +4.0%QoQ), 영업이
익은 6,098억원(-3.7%YoY, +4.0%QoQ, OPM 44.4%)으로 컨센서스 매출액 1
조 3,450억원과 영업이익 6,063억원에 부합하는 실적을 전망한다.  
5월 파업 영향으로 약 20개 배치 생산분(약 1,500억원)에 대한 원가 손실이 인식되
고 3분기 평균 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락했음에도 불구하고, 3분기부터 매출로 
인식되는 5공장 PPQ 배치 및 미국 공장의 매출 기여로 지속적인 성장이 예상된다. 약 
1,500억원 규모의 생산 차질분은 고객사와 일정 조율을 통해 출하될 예정으로 이에 
따른 4분기 매출 성장을 기대해 볼 수 있다.   
신규 수주 회복과 모달리티 확장에 주목 
시장에서 우려했던 신규 수주는 관세 이슈로 고객사의 컨택이 감소하면서 올해 저조했
으나 4 월 Section 232 결과 발표 이후 논의가 회복 단계에 있어 연내 추가 신규 수주 
가능성은 열려 있다.  
항체 시장의 경쟁심화와 차세대 모달리티 확장에 대응하기 위해 결정한 폴리펩타이드
의 인수는 연내 마무리될 예정이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2028 년 목표 EBITDA 마진은 
25%로 동사 대비 낮은 상황이나, 펩타이드 기반 CDMO 시장 성장에 따른 마진 개선
도 가능하다. 현재 임단협, 환율, 미국 공장 및 폴리펩타이드 인수에 따른 회계 영향 등 
불확실성이 큰 상황이나, 수주 회복과 5 공장 매출 인식 본격화, 미국 공장 및 멀티 모
달리티 확장 등 중장기 성장 방향성에 주목할 필요가 있다.

## Document 261: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2 분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2 분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4 분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As expected, the RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) lifted
the cash rate 25bps to 4.85% at its November meeting.
This follows the increase at the previous (late-September)
meeting and is a marked turnaround from the outlook back
in August. The post-meeting media statement cited higher

global energy prices resulting from the Middle East conflict,
tighter domestic capacity pressures and an increase in
inflation expectations for the decision.
However, the decision was a split vote, 6 votes to 3, a
possibility we have flagged for some time. In the post-meeting
media conference, Governor Bullock explained that the
dissenting minority put more weight on the downside risks and
did not see signs of wages growth picking up as much as the

staff forecasts implied. Those members were reportedly more
confident that slower growth would prevent higher inflation
expectations becoming entrenched.
Financial conditions are restrictive and this is expected to
bring inflation down over time. Because the RBA assesses the
risks to the inflation outlook to still be skewed to the upside,
the outlook for coming meetings will still be for a decision
between holding and hiking. But we note that the post-meeting

statement omitted September’s language “including increasing
the cash rate target further if needed” from the commitment to
do what is necessary to get inflation down.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor
commented that after five hikes, this is probably a good time
to “take stock”. Together with the dissents, we read this as
confirming our current view that the MPB will keep rates
on hold in December. There is also considerable data flow

between now and then, which could go either way, including
the Q3 national accounts.
Similarly, a lot could happen between now and February, but
we see it as the next most likely timing for a hike, should one
eventuate. We still see that as a risk rather than a base case.
Revised forecasts published in the accompanying Statement
on Monetary Policy (SMP) show underlying inflation lingering
around current rates for longer. This contrasts with the
previous SMP forecasts in August, which showed trimmed

mean inflation returning to the 2–3%yr target range by late
2027 and a little below the 2.5%yr target midpoint in 2028.
We note, however, that the conditioning assumptions on oil
prices, which are based on futures prices, were at a local peak
at the time the forecasts were finalised.
RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The RBA’s GDP growth forecasts for 2027 and 2028 were
both revised down –0.3%pts to 1.3% for 2027 and 1.5% in

2028, despite an upward revision to the near term. This is
well below even the RBA’s downbeat view of potential output
growth. Within the GDP aggregate, recent misses and leading
indicators have required upward revisions to the near-term
outlooks for business and dwelling investment, and downside
revisions to public demand. These revisions were offset further
out with downward revisions to the 2028 outlook for business
and dwelling investment and an upward revision to public

demand. Household consumption forecasts were revised down
across most of the profile.
With the unemployment rate already at 4.7% in August and
September – a rate the RBA did not forecast until end-2027 in
its August SMP forecasts – the RBA has lifted its unemployment
forecast to show a peak of 5.1% in at end-2028, the end of the
forecast horizon. This largely reflects stronger labour supply,
with forecasts for employment growth essentially unchanged.

We have repeatedly flagged that cost-of-living pressures are
likely to keep people in the labour force and thus boost the
participation rate. The RBA had previously not factored this
adequately into its forecasts but acknowledged in the SMP
that this mechanism had kept participation higher than it had
expected at the time of the August SMP.
However, because inflation surprised to the upside recently,
this additional labour supply has had no bearing on the

RBA’s assessment of capacity pressures. The RBA assesses
the domestic economy to still be a little tight, and tighter
than previously judged. The RBA’s framework for assessing
the degree of spare capacity or tightness in the economy
necessarily translates an upside surprise on inflation (or unit
labour costs) into a conclusion that the economy must be
tighter than previously judged. Indeed, the RBA’s estimate
of the NAIRU (unemployment rate consistent with inflation

stable at target) has been revised up to 4.75%.
The RBA highlighted that elevated inflation expectations may
be contributing to higher realised inflation. Consumer inflation
expectations have started to lift again but remain below where
they peaked earlier this year. However, the RBA appears
“RBA lifts cash rate 25bps to 4.85% in split
vote, citing “broadly based” domestic inflation
pressures, Middle East conflict and AI boom."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
to be putting more attention on the inflation expectations
of firms and the frequency of mentions of pricing in their
liaison program. This is putting a lot of weight on proprietary
information that is not clearly validated by other, longer-
running data sources.

The AI boom is heavily cited in the SMP and other post-
meeting communication. The RBA has lifted its forecasts
for major trading partners, especially high-income Asian
economies that are the centre of high-tech manufacturing.
Demand for AI-related goods is also cited as a driver of both
global and domestic inflation. And although the RBA has
revised up its near-term forecasts for business investment
on the back of strong data centre activity, it is working on

the assumption that Australia does not become ‘a significant
regional hub for data centres’. Nor does it allow for any boost
to productivity trends from the technology, even in 2028. This
is a very different view of the risks from AI than the RBNZ
expressed last week. For the RBA, it is all about inflation, a
little bit about pressure on domestic construction resources,
and nothing about either an ugly unwind of the current
financing boom or a future productivity dividend.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: whiplash
total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of
Australia’s data-centre investment boom, which should feature
prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3
National Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term

elections. In the House, Senate and a series of State Governor
races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come
2027, the Democrats will have a comfortable majority in both
houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-
of-living pressures. The election result also limits President
Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against
Iran. Notably, both immediately before and after the election,

President Trump and the administration have exhibited a greater
interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not
a surprise that Brent oil has backed off its most recent highs
around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to
near USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since
traded back up to above USD99, still 16% below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a
robust broad-based rally in major equity indexes, concerns

over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related
infrastructure buildout jolted US equities lower overnight – the
Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of
the prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and
FOMC rate expectations have also been scaled back; so, while
tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better
outcomes across the rest of the US economy may become
evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest

their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in
the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s concerns over US inflation
should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not
hike in December is a high bar, but the probability of further
hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate
by 25bps to 4.00% at their November meeting as expected.
On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to

rise from 3.5%yr at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity
data has also surprised to the upside in recent months, though
"there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other
prices and wages".
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase
at its November meeting, taking the cash rate to 4.85%. The
move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued
concern around inflation following the recent run higher in
global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation
is now not expected to return to the mid-point of the
target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth
expectations and the unemployment rate trending up to
5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with
three members preferring to remain on hold given downside
risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth.
Governor Bullock’s suggestion that it was time to “take stock”

after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference
to raising rates further “if needed”. In our view, February
represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but
we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible.
Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in October to now
be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline
is approaching the scale of previous housing corrections but

appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals  saw a 5.1% headline gain in
September, but this was driven by volatile unit approvals while
detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates
and the established-market downturn are beginning to weigh
on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending  meanwhile
rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3; however, much of
the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly

for fuel. Real spending volumes expanded by a much more
modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at
income, spending and saving flows. The latest update suggests
that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are
increasingly being squeezed, particularly those with low savings
buffers. With another rate increase now delivered, housing
wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like

the high-water mark for growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus
widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-monetary gold trade
yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The
most notable development was another exceptionally strong
result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the Q3
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
“The RBA and Bank of England both tightened

policy this week, in part due to price pressures
emanating from the Middle East."

In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the
nascent improvement in energy price pressures over the
past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but
also conveyed that "Unless progress towards resolving the
conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk

to inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may
be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and comments made in
the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this
was a decision that needs to be made in December, but rather
a risk to be judged as additional information is received. This
points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027
than before, and only if conditions / risks warrant.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
No deals are done
This week’s developments underscore our message that
a rate hike at the RBNZ’s December meeting is far from a
done deal. New Zealand’s labour market remains softer
than expected, and that will be noted by those on the

policy committee who question the extent of second-round
inflation pressures. The rout in the US mid-term elections
has also taken significant heat out of oil prices for now,
although it’s unclear whether that situation will hold through
to next month. Our view on the endpoint for this OCR cycle
remains unchanged, but there’s a growing risk that the RBNZ
could slow the pace of tightening in the new year.
The September quarter labour market surveys pointed to

ongoing softness in the jobs market. The unemployment
rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing a four-year run
of increases, and reaching its highest level since 2015.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth
remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-living pressures.
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise
0.1% fall in employment in the September quarter, after a
downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That

went against the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure
of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward momentum
emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure
had outpaced the MEI for the previous nine straight quarters).
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in
the labour force participation rate from 70.6% to 70.4%.
These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly
large increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had

suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error
in the survey rather than a genuine surge.
We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate,
which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message
here is one of softness. Our best interpretation is that, to the
extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the
growth in the population (which itself is a fairly low bar, as net
migration remains at a low ebb).

Even with the economy returning to growth over the last
year or so, hiring activity has remained weak. This may be
an ongoing legacy of employment trends in previous years.
Businesses have told us that they felt burned by the severe
skill shortages that developed over 2021-23, and looked to
hold on to workers to the extent they could during the 2024-
25 downturn. The flipside of this is that they may now find
themselves overstaffed even as activity picks up again.

This shows up most strikingly in labour force transition rates.
The rise in unemployment over the last few years has not
been driven by an increase in job losses, but by accumulation
of people who, once out of work, are finding it increasingly
difficult to get back in again. That also extends to young
people trying to enter the workforce for the first time – youth
unemployment of around 25% is the highest in the history of
the survey going back to 1986.

The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September
quarter, in line with our expectations overall but a bit weaker
in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while
there was a surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector
wages. There was a notable jump in the number of employers
citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay
rates – although the size of those increases varied a great deal,
with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate.

Potential RBNZ implications
The labour market data was unequivocally weaker than
expected by either us or the RBNZ. While likely not a
gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the
arguments of the doves on the Monetary Policy Committee that
a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon – perhaps in
December or early next year. It’s likely the case that interest rate
markets were over-pricing the odds of a hike at the 9 December

MPS, so the subsequent pullback in pricing makes sense.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting was to
be careful about pricing in "done deals", and that the outlook
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside
measures of inflation expectations, the key domestic data
to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the
output gap), as this will be a key determinant of the Bank’s

assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
The RBNZ looks at a broad suite of indicators of labour market
slack, although their research found that only four of them
seem to have predictive power: the unemployment rate,
the vacancy to unemployment ratio, the QSBO measure of
labour as the main limiting factor to growth, and the rate of
transition between jobs. The first three of these all pointed
to a softening in the September quarter. (The fourth one is

calculated from income tax data, so it’s not publicly available
and even the RBNZ receives it only with a lag.)
In the eyes of the dovish MPC members, the softness in
the labour market indicators will likely push in the direction
of having greater comfort with the medium-term inflation
outlook. The outcome of the election, with the possibility of a
return to a dual inflation/employment mandate, could increase
the prominence of these factors. This likely won’t be an explicit

factor in the RBNZ’s reasoning at its December review, but it
may be in the minds of individual MPC members.
That said, it’s important to remember that in the RBNZ’s
forecasting framework, the GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy. The most
recent GDP data tells us that the output gap probably
narrowed a bit through the June quarter, mainly reflecting
historical revisions. In light of the latest labour market figures,

there may be some downside risk to the outlook for Q3 GDP –
but there are conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear.
At last month’s surprise on-hold OCR decision, Governor
Breman lamented that there was no protocol for the RBNZ
to be able to signal how it was reading the economic data –
in particular the softer than expected Q3 CPI print – ahead
of the policy meeting. In that light, it will be interesting to
see whether they use some already-scheduled speaking

engagements to signal what they make of the labour market
data. Top of mind is Chief Economist Conway's 10 November
speech to the UBS Australasia Conference (pointedly titled
“How is the NZ economy tracking”). Governor Breman is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November.
Our view remains that the December OCR hike is more likely
than not. But there will be a clear debate to be had between
the MPC hawks and doves, and it may again come down to a

vote. We still expect a 4% peak in the OCR for this cycle, but
the time taken to get there could be more flexible. Takes along
the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" seem rather hasty on the
basis of a single data release.
Aside from how the Committee interprets the labour market
data, the key considerations in December will be: the RBNZ’s
surveys of inflation expectations by households and businesses;
whether the US mid-term elections lead to a sustained de-

escalation in the Middle East and a pullback in oil prices; and
whether developments in the AI space exacerbate some MPC
members’ concerns about the global economic environment.
And then of course there’s the result of the General Election,
with voting closing on Saturday evening. The actual formation
of a government is likely to take weeks of negotiation, so the
outcome may not be known by the 9 December MPS but at least
the range of possibilities should be clearer. We’ll publish our

initial thoughts on the election results early on Monday morning.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Oct Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Nov 12, Last: –0.4, Westpac f/c: 1.4
We’re forecasting a 1.4% rise in retail spending in October.
While that would be a solid increase, it’s in large part due to
the 11% rise in petrol prices over the past month. Stripping out

fuel spending, we’re forecasting a 0.6% rise in core spending
categories. Core spending has been very choppy recently,
and the increase we’re assuming follows falls in the past few
months. A result in line with our expectations would leave the
trend in core spending relatively flat since the start of this year
with the rise in fuel prices weighing on spending in other areas.
AUS: Nov Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Nov 10, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment fell 4.7% to 80.4 in Oct, returning to the

deeply pessimistic levels seen earlier in the year. Responses
over the course of the survey week showed a very sharp
deterioration after the RBA rate hike.
The Nov survey is in the field over the week ended Nov 7.
Interest rates will again factor with the RBA raising the cash
rate by another 25bps to 4.85%, an 18yr high. Average pump
prices also pushed higher, the national average just under
$2.40/litre, while the housing market downturn has continued

to broaden and deepen. The US mid-term election results may
have some effects on sentiment locally as well.
AUS: Q3 Housing Finance Approvals (%qtr)
Nov 11 Last: –5.2, Westpac f/c: –10
Market f/c: –8.5, Range: –15 to –5
The total value of housing finance approvals fell The value of
new dwelling finance approvals excl. refinance fell 5.2%qtr,
the largest quarterly drop in three years. Annual growth eased
to 7.3%yr. The weakness was accounted for mainly by lower

lending volumes: loan numbers were down 4.0%qtr (–0.6%yr),
while average loan size also contributed negatively.
Q3 is expected to see an even bigger decline with the full
impact of interest rate rises and tax policy changes showing
through. The value of both loan enquiries and turnover fell by
around 10% in the quarter. We expect total finance approvals
to show a 10% drop as well, with investor loan approvals likely
to be down 16%qtr and owner occupier approvals down 6%qtr.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
US: Oct CPI (%mth)
Nov 11, Headline, Last: 0.6, Market f/c: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.4
US consumer prices rose by 0.6% in September, although
half of the gain was due to energy inflation, with core prices
instead up 0.3%. Over the year, prices were reported to have

risen 3.7%yr overall and 2.6%yr excluding food and energy.
Constructively, ex food, energy and shelter, annual inflation
met the FOMC’s medium-term target of 2.0%yr.
Come October, energy price pressures are set to recede
given the pre-election run higher in oil prices came late in the
month. A softer print for shelter is also likely to moderate core
momentum. We see a 0.4% gain for the headline measure and
a 0.2% result for core inflation as most probable. Looking to

2027, disinflation is expected to continue at a measured pace
towards the medium-term target.

## Document 343: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-06).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

## Document 426: Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price_ _ GSTW

Source PDF: all report pdf/Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price_ _ GSTW.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings
Still Enough to Drive Stock Price? | GSTW
30 October 2026 | 4:17 PM Taiwan Standard Time
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
Short week but busy for Taiwan in terms of earnings. Index is only down 1.8% WTD,
though multiple large caps saw over 10% drawdown in this week alone. The elevated
level in Taiwan vs other regional markets still attracted much short interest without

much buying interest except from retail. Overall sentiment is not bearish, though
investors have turned much more defensive and hiding in sectors such as Financials,
Telecom, and TSMC.
For earnings, winners' momentum was short lived; instead of chasing into the beat,
many chose to further profit take on the strength (Unimicron, EMC/TUC, Landmark
on open right after their earnings). Companies that missed the results were definitely
punished (i.e. Chroma, Delta, UMC). Echoing back to my previous note, range bound

trade seems to be the safest way to stay afloat.
See below for my single stock takeaway:
Mediatek (2454 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$168.06bn (+10% QoQ and +18% YoY) with EPS at
NT$17.5 (+15% QoQ and +11% YoY) vs cons. NT$15.95. GM came in at 46.3%
(+11ppt QoQ and 18ppt YoY) vs cons. 46.2%.
3Q26 outperformance was mainly from stronger-than-expected customer
demand and improved supply chain yield. First ASIC already started shipping since

Sep'26.
Operational Outlook:
Revenue outlook for 4Q26 is NT$210 - NT$222bn, translating to a 29% sequential
growth. 2027 revenue growth will likely grow by high single digits yoy. GM will be
around 45% with +/-1.5%, and OPM is expected to be at 26% +/-2%.
ASIC chip sales for 2026 will be around US$2.5bn, higher than previous guidance,
and it will reach US$16bn in 2027, around 20% of ASIC TAM and at the higher end
of the company guidance. Next gen chip's tape out is on track and targeted to

enter mass production in 1Q28.
Delta Electronics (2308 TT)
10/30/26, 4:46 PM Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price? | GSTW
https://marquee.gs.com/s/content/markets/2971c821-c2cc-403a-acfe-2b906dd125c4 1/11

### Page 2

Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$197.1bn (+8% QoQ / +31% YoY), while EPS registered
at NT$9.05. GM declined to 34.1% (down 1.5ppt QoQ), impacted by a NT$3.9bn
inventory write-down (~1.7% of revenue) mostly in Power Electronics.
Operating profit rose +7% QoQ (+31% YoY) backed by operating leverage, keeping
OPM resilient at 16.6% flat QoQ. Net profit was NT$23.5bn (-7% QoQ / +26% YoY)
due to lower non-operating investment gains.
Operational Outlook:

Management expects 4Q26 revenue to grow sequentially with gross margins
unlikely to deteriorate, aided by inventory normalization and potential price
adjustments to offset ~2% YTD material cost inflation.
AI-related exposure approaches 50% of total revenue (liquid cooling at ~13%), and
management remains confident in AI infrastructure demand through 2030 despite
near-term bottlenecks.
Key Q&A:
Inventory Write-down & Normalization: The NT$3.9bn write-down stemmed from

component mismatches and slower customer ramp-ups of newer platforms (e.g.,
18.5kW vs. 5.5kW platforms); management expects affected inventory to be
progressively consumed by 1Q27.
HVDC Adoption & Vicor Litigation: While 400V products saw partial shipments in
3Q26, 800V adoption is slower and primarily NVIDIA-driven. On the Vicor VPD
patent dispute, management expects no near-term impact on customer
relationships or shipments, with an initial ITC determination unlikely until 2027.

ASE Technology Holding (3711 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 consolidated revenue reached NT$244.16bn, with a GM of 22.1% and EPS of
NT$5.84, all outperforming market expectations.
ATM (Advanced Semiconductor Engineering, Test and Materials) GM hit 30.6%,
breaking past previous structural highs; the company announced 30% as its
baseline gross margin for the future.
Operational Outlook:
4Q26 consolidated revenue is projected to grow 6%–8% QoQ, with GM between

23.0%–24.0% and OPM between 14.5%–15.5%.
CPO is set to enter mass production in 4Q26, with related revenue expected to
contribute over US$500mn in 2027; the full-year LEAP revenue target has been
revised upward to over US$4bn.
Key Q&A:
Capex Upward Revision: Due to strong demand for LEAP and mainstream
advanced packaging, full-year 2026 capex was raised from US$10.5bn to
US$11.5bn (60% allocated to LEAP, 40% to mainstream packaging), with 2027
capex expected to be even higher.

Capacity Bottlenecks & Expansion: Factory floor space is currently the primary
constraint on near-term growth. The company is actively searching for and
acquiring existing facilities to shorten setup timelines, prioritizing resource
allocation toward packaging and wafer sort.
Elite Material (2383 TT)
10/30/26, 4:46 PM Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price? | GSTW
https://marquee.gs.com/s/content/markets/2971c821-c2cc-403a-acfe-2b906dd125c4 2/11

### Page 3

Financial Highlights:
Driven by strong demand, actual 3Q26 revenue grew 29% QoQ, comfortably
beating prior conservative expectations.
GM outperformed expectations, hitting 37.7% for the quarter.
Operational Outlook:
4Q26 revenue growth is conservatively estimated to be at least 15%–20% QoQ,
with gross margins expanding further to 38.0%–39.5%.
Proactive capacity expansion plans (targeting 15.25 million sheets/month by end-
2030 vs. 5.85 million sheets/month currently) are still insufficient to support all

signed Long-Term Agreements (LTAs) for the next 4 years, pointing to a severe
supply-demand gap and zero oversupply risk.
Key Q&A:
CCL Pricing & Substrate Expansion: EMC is executing its second round of high-end
CCL (M7+) price hikes this year (5%–20% increases covering M7/M8 and M8+/M9
grades). Furthermore, it is rapidly expanding high-end ABF substrate core-layer
CCL capacity from 300k sheets/month to at least 1.2 million sheets/month by end-
2027, targeting commercial shipments starting in 4Q27.

Competition & Market Share: While AI customers are qualifying second or third
sources to secure supply chains, EMC maintains its long-term target of holding a
50% AI CCL market share, supported by best-in-class product quality and deep
CSP/PCB collaboration.
Yageo Corporation (2327 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$49.53bn (+11.4% QoQ, +49.7% YoY), with a GM of 41%
and EPS of NT$5.83.
Free cash flow surged to NT$9.236 billion, while the net financial debt-to-EBITDA

ratio dropped from 59% in 4Q25 to 24% in 3Q26, reflecting a continuously
improving financial structure and robust cash generation.
Operational Outlook:
4Q26 revenue, GM, and OPM are all expected to edge up sequentially, defying
typical seasonal flat-to-down trends for the fourth quarter.
AI demand remains solid, with its contribution to total revenue expanding from
15% in 1Q26 and 16% in 2Q26 to approximately 20% in 3Q26.
Key Q&A:
Capacity & Expansion Plans: Capacity utilization rates stood at 95% in 3Q26 and

are expected to exceed 95% in 4Q26, surpassing the healthy range of 85%–90%.
Total capacity for tantalum capacitors and MLCCs is planned to expand by 10%–
15% in 2027, with portions coming online starting in the second quarter.
Order Momentum & B/B Ratio: The overall book-to-bill (B/B) ratio in 3Q26
remained close to 2.0, with AI-related tantalum capacitors and MLCCs seeing
particularly robust orders, while LTAs with core customers continue to underpin

future shipments.
Nan Ya Plastics (1303 TT)
Financial Highlights:

3Q26 revenue rose to NT$95.6bn (+14.3% QoQ), with electronic materials

TUC (6274 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 financial results beat market consensus, reporting revenue of NT17.95bn,
GM of 36.9%, operating margin of 31.0%, and EPS of NT$12.88, outperforming

consensus estimates by 29% to 35%.

first-half losses.
Operational Outlook:
For 4Q26, high-end product pricing is initiating upward adjustments. With the
Thailand plant adding another 100k sheets of capacity and all series of CCL seeing
price hike opportunities (especially high-end M7/M8 catch-up price increases

alongside tightening mid-to-low-end fiberglass cloth supply), product mix and
profitability are set to improve further.
ASIC AI servers are emerging as a core growth engine, alongside long-term
opportunities from satellite and space data centers. TUC is adding several large
ASIC customers (some already in sampling/board qualification stages) and
expanding its customer base in 400G, 800G, and 1.6T switches.
Key Q&A / Detailed Highlights:
ASIC & Switch Expansion: AI servers will serve as the primary growth driver, with

multiple major ASIC clients entering the board-maker certification stage;
meanwhile, existing 400G switch customers are rapidly accelerating 800G and 1.6T
switch certifications, with some expecting 1.6T demand to outpace 800G by 2027.
Satellite and PTFE Solutions: Space satellite PCBs utilizing M8+ materials are slated
to begin contribution in 1H27, with long-term customer demand forecasts already
extended through 2032. For PTFE, TUC boasts ~5–6 years of R&D foundation

rather than being a newcomer, though external partnerships may be pursued to
bypass special high-temperature press machine capacity limitations

realize each day a loss or a gain which is a multiple (for leveraged ETFs and ETNs) or the inverse (for inverse ETFs and ETNs) of the

performance on that day of their underlying index or benchmark. For this reason, if they are held for a period longer than one day, their
performance over such longer periods of time can differ significantly from the stated multiple of the performance (or the inverse of the
performance) of their underlying index or benchmark during the same period of time. This effect can be magnified in volatile markets.
Prior to entering into a transaction in leveraged or inverse ETFs, you should be aware of the general risks associated with such

transactions. You should not enter into leveraged or inverse ETFs transactions unless you understand the nature and extent of your risk
exposure. You should also be satisfied that the leveraged or inverse ETFs transaction is appropriate for you in light of your circumstances
and financial condition. ETFs and ETNs that are linked to commodity futures do not offer direct exposure to the commodity's spot price,
and may perform differently than the spot price for the commodity itself. Performance differential can be magnified if a specific

condition persists in the market for a commodity that creates a disparity between near-term future prices and long-term future prices
and may lead to unexpected performance results. Other factors, such as roll yield, transaction costs, management fees, and taxes may
cause deviation in performance between the spot price of a commodity and commodity futures. You should not assume that an ETF or
ETN that is linked to commodity futures will provide an effective hedge because of a negative correlation with equities or other asset

swaps, and other derivatives as well as non-investment-grade securities - give rise to substantial risk and are not available to nor suitable
for all investors. If you have any questions about whether you are eligible to enter into these transactions with Goldman Sachs, please

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投

東協維持整理，印度新契機浮現
分析師／王聖堯
研究範疇／東協/印度
東南亞投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/1
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《東協尚未擺脫整理，美印關係改善訊號浮現》
9月MSCI東協指數下跌 2.6%，雖然全球公債殖利率
與油價走升帶來評價面壓力 ，但從股價跌幅及外資
賣超規模來看 ，本波修正仍偏向短線漲多後的技術
性調節。修正後，東協股市本益比已由高於近兩年
平均2倍標準差回落至約 1倍標準差 ，RSI亦降至40
以下，顯示市場過熱情況明顯改善 。然而在聯準會
升息及殖利率維持高檔的背景下 ，若基本面未能提
供更多上修空間 ，預期股市短期仍將維持震盪整理
格局。印度方面，9月回檔已使股價回測 3月低點，
本益比降至 19.9倍，RSI跌破30，顯示短線賣壓已

有過度反應。此外，莫迪於9月金磚峰會公開反對去
美元化，近期川普亦表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，反映
美印經貿關係出現改善訊號 。後續可持續關注雙邊
合作進展及其對印度中長期投資影響。

《外資9月轉為小幅賣超，呼應8月轉中立看法》
8月下調東協評等至中立 ，主要考量 6至8月股市累
積漲幅已大致反映第三季經濟維持穩健成長的預期 ，
在缺乏新的催化劑下 ，市場進一步上行空間受限 。
進入9月後，美國公債殖利率與國際油價同步走升，
使市場重新評估高利率與成本壓力對企業獲利的影
響，並加大非科技類股及非科技市場的修正幅度 。
不過，相較於美國公債殖利率持續創高所帶來的壓
力，東協股市 9月僅下跌 2.6%，跌幅仍屬溫和；從
資金面來看 ，外資雖轉為淨賣超 ，但賣超規模明顯
低於第二季調整期間 ，顯示本波修正較偏向漲多後
的獲利了結與資產重新配置 ，而非反映市場對區域
基本面的明顯轉弱或出現恐慌性撤資。

《出口支撐成長敘事未變 ，但高通膨也比預期持久 》
東協及印度經濟仍展現韌性，多數國家8月出口維持
雙位數成長且高於長期平均 ，與全球製造業擴張趨
勢相符 ，其中新加坡及馬來西亞受惠 AI強勁資本支
出，8月出口成長年增率逾 5成，顯示外需仍是重要
成長支撐 。強勁的出口與製造活動不僅有助支撐企
業獲利 ，也在一定程度上抵銷高利率對消費與投資
的壓力 。另一方面 ，印尼、菲律賓等先前高通膨國
家雖已進入降溫趨勢 ，但通膨回落速度仍較緩慢 ，
使央行在降息時程上維持審慎 。整體而言 ，目前東
協與印度景氣尚未出現明顯轉弱 ，但高利率環境可
能維持較市場預期更久 ，意味短期經濟成長動能將
更仰賴外需及製造業活動的支撐。

• 東協及印度出口維持強勁成

速度偏慢 ，恐使高利率環境

《川普表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，重構印度樞紐角色 》
川普近期表態支持印歐經濟走廊 (IMEC)，使這項拜
登任內支持，但因加薩戰爭與區域衝突而停滯近3年
的計畫再度受到市場關注 。隨著中東地緣風險升溫 ，
紅海及荷姆茲海峽等關鍵航道不確定性提高 ，各國
對替代性能源與運輸路徑的需求同步增加 。從規劃
路線來看 ，印歐經濟走廊以印度為起點 ，串連海灣
產油國與歐洲市場 ，若計畫順利推進 ，除有助印度
取得更穩定的中東能源供給 、降低對俄羅斯石油的
依賴外 ，也將進一步鞏固其作為全球製造與供應鏈
樞紐的地位 。對印股而言 ，IMEC有望同時強化能源
安全與供應鏈地位 ，鞏固印度在全球製造的地位 ，
並為基建、工業及製造類股帶來中長期題材支撐。

重點摘要
• 川普表態支持擱置近 3年的印
歐經濟走廊重啟 ，在中東地
緣風險升溫下 ，有助印度強
化能源安全 、降低對俄油依
賴，並鞏固全球製造與供應
鏈樞紐地位。
